--- TOOL: grep ---
{"pattern":"bin\\\\x64\\\\Debug|AppPackages|TargetFramework|RuntimeIdentifier|winapp run|Build output","paths":"C:\\Users\\xiaomgao\\AppData\\Local\\win-dev-bench\\repos\\DinahK-2SO__win-dev-skills__user--DinahK-2SO--uwp2winui3-autoevolve-260702-172138-085\\plugins\\winui-uwp-migration\\skills\\winui-uwp-migration\\scripts\\Test-AppLaunch.ps1","output_mode":"content","-n":true,"head_limit":120}
--- RESULT ---
1. <#
2. .SYNOPSIS
3. Launch an already-built WinUI 3 app and report whether it survives startup -
4. and if not, capture the REAL unhandled-exception signature from Windows Error
5. Reporting so a startup crash can be diagnosed immediately instead of guessed at.
6. 
7. .DESCRIPTION
8. A packaged WinUI 3 app can build cleanly and still crash on startup. When that
9. happens the only durable signal is the Windows Application event log: event 1000
10. (faulting module + native exception code) and event 1026 (.NET Runtime - the
11. managed exception TYPE, message, and stack). This script launches the built
12. layout via 'winapp run --detach --json', waits for the cold-start window, and:
13. 
14.   - if the process is alive  -> status=running  (exit 0)
15.   - if it registered then died/threw at startup -> status=crashed (exit 1),
16.     with the captured WER signature + a pointer to PATTERNS.md#startup-crashes
17.   - if it never registered (deploy/env failure: Developer Mode off, missing
18.     framework, MAX_PATH, no winapp) -> status=unavailable (exit 2) - inconclusive,
19.     NOT a code defect.
20. 
21. It NEVER throws on an operational failure; it returns a structured result and
22. (with -Json) prints it as JSON. This is the single source of truth for the
23. "did the migrated app launch" question - both Step 3 (interactive, when the app
24. first crashes) and the Step 4 validator (Section 7) call it.
25. 
26. WHY THIS MATTERS: the common failure mode is an agent that sees a startup crash
27. and hand-rolls File.WriteAllText tracing for the rest of its budget. Don't. Run
28. this once; read the captured managed exception; fix the named frame.
29. 
30. NOTE: this script is intentionally ASCII-only. The Step 4 validator invokes it
31. via 'powershell -File' (Windows PowerShell 5.1), which reads a UTF-8-no-BOM file
32. as ANSI; non-ASCII characters would corrupt parsing under 5.1.
33. 
34. .PARAMETER Target
35. The migrated WinUI 3 project root (the folder with the .csproj). The build-output
36. layout is discovered under bin/<arch>/Debug/<tfm>/win-<rid>. Use this OR -Layout.
37. 
38. .PARAMETER Layout
39. An explicit build-output layout folder (the one containing AppxManifest.xml +
40. the app .exe, or an AppX\ subfolder). Use this OR -Target.
41. 
42. .PARAMETER SettleSeconds
43. Seconds to wait after launch before the alive check. Default 10 - enough to
44. catch OnLaunched / first-Page-navigation crashes without bloating runtime.
45. 
46. .PARAMETER Json
47. Emit the structured result as JSON to stdout (for programmatic callers).
48. 
49. .OUTPUTS
50. PSCustomObject (and JSON with -Json):
51.   { ok, status, pid, aumid, layout, crash:{ code, module, managedType, message,
52.     hint, anchor } | $null, detail }
53. status in { running, crashed, unavailable }.  exit: 0 running / 1 crashed / 2 unavailable.
54. 
55. .EXAMPLE
56. .\Test-AppLaunch.ps1 -Target "C:\out\MyWinUI3App"
57. # Interactive: launch and, on crash, print the real exception + the fix pointer.
58. 
59. .EXAMPLE
60. $r = .\Test-AppLaunch.ps1 -Layout $launchFolder -Json | ConvertFrom-Json
61. if ($r.status -eq 'crashed') { "FAIL: $($r.crash.hint)" }
62. #>
63. [CmdletBinding(DefaultParameterSetName = 'Target')]
64. param(
65.     [Parameter(Mandatory, ParameterSetName = 'Target')][string]$Target,
66.     [Parameter(Mandatory, ParameterSetName = 'Layout')][string]$Layout,
67.     [int]$SettleSeconds = 10,
68.     [switch]$Json
69. )
70. 
71. # This script reports failures structurally; it must not abort on a non-zero
72. # external tool exit. Errors are handled explicitly per stage.
73. $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'
74. 
75. function New-LaunchResult {
76.     param([bool]$Ok, [string]$Status, [string]$Detail, [hashtable]$Extra)
77.     $r = [ordered]@{
78.         ok = $Ok; status = $Status; pid = $null; aumid = $null
79.         layout = $null; crash = $null; detail = $Detail
80.     }
81.     if ($Extra) { foreach ($k in $Extra.Keys) { $r[$k] = $Extra[$k] } }
82.     [pscustomobject]$r
83. }
84. 
85. function Write-LaunchOut {
86.     param([pscustomobject]$Result)
87.     # exit code: running=0, crashed=1, unavailable=2
88.     $exit = switch ($Result.status) { 'running' { 0 } 'crashed' { 1 } default { 2 } }
89.     if ($Json) {
90.         $Result | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 6
91.     } else {
92.         $tag = switch ($Result.status) { 'running' { 'OK  ' } 'crashed' { 'FAIL' } default { 'WARN' } }
93.         Write-Host ""
94.         Write-Host "==> Test-AppLaunch [$tag] status=$($Result.status)"
95.         if ($Result.layout) { Write-Host "    Layout : $($Result.layout)" }
96.         if ($Result.pid)    { Write-Host "    PID    : $($Result.pid)   AUMID: $($Result.aumid)" }
97.         if ($Result.crash) {
98.             Write-Host "    Crash  : code=$($Result.crash.code)  module=$($Result.crash.module)"
99.             if ($Result.crash.managedType) { Write-Host "    .NET   : $($Result.crash.managedType)" }
100.             if ($Result.crash.message)     { Write-Host "    Message: $($Result.crash.message)" }
101.             if ($Result.crash.hint)        { Write-Host "    Hint   : $($Result.crash.hint)" }
102.             if ($Result.crash.anchor)      { Write-Host "    Fix    : Get-MigrationPattern.ps1 -Anchor $($Result.crash.anchor)" }
103.         }
104.         if ($Result.detail) { Write-Host "    Detail : $($Result.detail)" }
105.     }
106.     exit $exit
107. }
108. 
109. # ---- Resolve the launch layout ----------------------------------------------
110. $exeNames = @()
111. if ($PSCmdlet.ParameterSetName -eq 'Target') {
112.     if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Target)) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "Target not found: $Target") }
113.     $Target = (Resolve-Path -LiteralPath $Target).Path
114.     $csproj = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $Target -Recurse -Filter *.csproj -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
115.         Where-Object { $_.FullName -notmatch '\\(bin|obj|\.uwp-source)\\' } | Select-Object -First 1 -ExpandProperty FullName
116.     if (-not $csproj) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "No .csproj found under $Target") }
117.     $csprojDir = Split-Path -Parent $csproj
118.     $arch = if ($env:PROCESSOR_ARCHITECTURE -eq 'ARM64') { 'ARM64' } else { 'x64' }
119.     $rid = $arch.ToLower()
120.     # Walk bin/<arch>/Debug and one level into the newest TFM dir, then win-<rid>.
121.     $binCandidates = @(
122.         (Join-Path $csprojDir "bin\$arch\Debug"),
123.         (Join-Path $csprojDir "bin\$rid\Debug")
124.     )
125.     foreach ($bin in $binCandidates) {
126.         if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $bin)) { continue }
127.         $tfmDir = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $bin -Directory -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
128.             Sort-Object LastWriteTime -Descending | Select-Object -First 1
129.         if (-not $tfmDir) { continue }
130.         $ridDir = Join-Path $tfmDir.FullName "win-$rid"
131.         $Layout = if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $ridDir) { $ridDir } else { $tfmDir.FullName }
132.         break
133.     }
134.     if (-not $Layout) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "No build output found under bin\$arch\Debug - build the project first.") }
135. }
136. 
137. if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Layout)) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "Layout folder not found: $Layout") }
138. $Layout = (Resolve-Path -LiteralPath $Layout).Path
139. 
140. # Sanity: the layout needs an exe + a manifest, else winapp run can't launch it.
141. $exes = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $Layout -Filter '*.exe' -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)
142. $exeNames = @($exes | ForEach-Object { [System.IO.Path]::GetFileNameWithoutExtension($_.Name) })
143. $hasMfst = (Test-Path -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Layout 'AppxManifest.xml')) -or
144.            (Test-Path -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Layout 'AppX\AppxManifest.xml'))
145. if ($exes.Count -eq 0 -or -not $hasMfst) {
146.     Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "Layout is incomplete (need an .exe + AppxManifest.xml): $Layout" @{ layout = $Layout })
147. }
148. if (-not (Get-Command winapp -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)) {
149.     Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "winapp CLI not on PATH - cannot launch the packaged app." @{ layout = $Layout })
150. }
151. 
152. # ---- Launch ------------------------------------------------------------------
153. $runRaw = & winapp run "$Layout" --detach --json 2>&1 | Out-String
154. $run = $null
155. try { $run = $runRaw.Trim() | ConvertFrom-Json -ErrorAction Stop } catch { }
156. $thePid = $null; $aumid = $null; $runErr = $null
157. if ($run) {
158.     foreach ($p in 'ProcessId','PID','Pid') { if ($run.$p) { $thePid = [int]$run.$p; break } }
159.     if ($run.AUMID) { $aumid = [string]$run.AUMID }
160.     if ($run.Error) { $runErr = [string]$run.Error }
161. }
162. 
163. # ---- WER capture helper ------------------------------------------------------
164. # Reads the real unhandled-exception signature for the just-crashed app:
165. #   event 1000  -> faulting module + native Exception code (e.g. 0x8001010E)
166. #   event 1026  -> .NET Runtime: managed exception TYPE + message + stack
167. # Match on the layout's exe base name(s) within a short recent window.
168. function Get-CrashSignature {
169.     param([string[]]$Names)
170.     $sig = [ordered]@{ code = $null; module = $null; managedType = $null; message = $null; hint = $null; anchor = $null }
171.     if (-not $Names -or $Names.Count -eq 0) { return $sig }
172.     $namePattern = ($Names | ForEach-Object { [regex]::Escape($_ + '.exe') }) -join '|'
173.     $events = Get-WinEvent -LogName Application -MaxEvents 80 -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
174.         Where-Object { $_.TimeCreated -gt (Get-Date).AddMinutes(-3) -and $_.Id -in 1000,1026 -and $_.Message -match $namePattern }
175. 
176.     $e1000 = $events | Where-Object { $_.Id -eq 1000 } | Select-Object -First 1
177.     if ($e1000) {
178.         if ($e1000.Message -match 'Faulting module name:\s*([^,]+)') { $sig.module = $matches[1].Trim() }
179.         if ($e1000.Message -match 'Exception code:\s*(0x[0-9a-fA-F]+)') { $sig.code = $matches[1].ToLower() }
180.     }
181.     $e1026 = $events | Where-Object { $_.Id -eq 1026 } | Select-Object -First 1
182.     if ($e1026) {
183.         # ".NET Runtime" event: "Exception Info: <Type>: <message>"
184.         if ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*([A-Za-z0-9_.]+Exception)') { $sig.managedType = $matches[1].Trim() }
185.         if ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*[A-Za-z0-9_.]+Exception[^\r\n:]*:\s*([^\r\n]+)') {
186.             $sig.message = $matches[1].Trim()
187.         } elseif ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*([^\r\n]+)') {
188.             $sig.message = $matches[1].Trim()
189.         }
190.     }
191. 
192.     # Map the native exception code to a conservative hint + pattern anchor.
193.     # These name the error CLASS and point at the captured stack / the doc; they
194.     # do not assert a single fix, since the real frame is in the captured exception.
195.     switch ($sig.code) {
196.         '0x80004003' {
197.             $sig.hint   = "E_POINTER - most often the static-window init-order race: a Page read App.MainWindow (or another static window ref) before OnLaunched assigned it. Keep MainWindow's ctor inert; navigate after Activate."
198.             $sig.anchor = 'windowing'
199.         }
200.         '0x8001010e' {
201.             $sig.hint   = "RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD - a thread/apartment-affined object was accessed during startup (commonly a view/CoreWindow-affined UWP API touched from a static initializer or off the UI thread). Construct/access it on the UI thread after Activate; if the API is unsupported on WinUI 3 desktop, defer it."
202.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
203.         }
204.         '0xe0434352' {
205.             $sig.hint   = "Managed .NET exception - see the .NET exception type captured above (event 1026). Resolve that type (e.g. TypeLoad / FileNotFound usually means a missing or incompatible package reference)."
206.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
207.         }
208.         '0xc000027b' {
209.             $sig.hint   = "Native stowed exception - frequently a legacy projection/activation incompatibility. If a UWP API/contract used at startup is unsupported on this OS, defer it per MIGRATION-DEFERRED.md."
210.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
211.         }
212.         default {
213.             $sig.hint   = "Startup crash - read the captured exception (event 1026) to find the throwing frame."
214.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
215.         }
216.     }
217.     return $sig
218. }
219. 
220. # ---- Classify ----------------------------------------------------------------
--- RESULT ---

<a id="startup-crashes"></a>
## Diagnosing Startup CrashesA packaged WinUI 3 app can build cleanly and still crash the instant it launches — `winapp run` returns no usable window and the process is gone. The build log and the analyzer cannot see this; only a real launch does. The **wrong** way to chase it is to sprinkle `File.WriteAllText` traces through `Program.cs` / `App.xaml.cs` and re-run repeatedly — that burns time and usually misses the real frame (the crash often happens in native activation, before your managed `Main` even runs).**Capture the real exception first.** Either run the launch-and-diagnose helper:```powershell
& "<skill-root>/scripts/Test-AppLaunch.ps1" -Target "<winui3-project-root>"
```…or read the Windows **Application** event log directly for the two events the crash writes:```powershell
Get-WinEvent -LogName Application -MaxEvents 40 |
  Where-Object { $_.Id -in 1000,1026 -and $_.TimeCreated -gt (Get-Date).AddMinutes(-3) } |
  ForEach-Object { "`n=== Event $($_.Id) @ $($_.TimeCreated) ==="; $_.Message }
```- **Event 1000** — faulting module + native **Exception code** (e.g. `0x8001010E`).
- **Event 1026** — *.NET Runtime*: the managed **exception type, message, and stack**. This is the one that names the actual throwing frame. Read it before changing any code.**Known native codes and what they usually mean** (the code names the error *class*; the real fix is whatever the captured stack points at):| Exception code | Meaning | Usual migration cause → where to look |
|---|---|---|
| `0x80004003` | `E_POINTER` | Static-window **init-order race** — a `Page` read `App.MainWindow` (or another static window reference) before `OnLaunched` assigned it. Keep `MainWindow`'s constructor inert and navigate after `Activate`. See [Initialization order](#windowing). |
| `0x8001010E` | `RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD` | A **thread/apartment-affined object** was accessed during startup — commonly a view- or `CoreWindow`-affined UWP API touched from a `static` initializer, a type constructor, or off the UI thread. Construct/access it on the UI thread *after* `Activate`. If the API has no WinUI 3 desktop equivalent, defer it. |
| `0xE0434352` | Managed CLR exception | Read the **.NET exception type** in event 1026. `TypeLoadException` / `FileNotFoundException` almost always means a missing or version-incompatible package reference, not your code. |
| `0xC000027B` | Native stowed exception | Often a legacy projection/activation incompatibility for an API used at startup. If the API/contract is unsupported on the current OS, defer it. |> Do **not** assume the entry point is the problem. A custom `Program.Main` for WinUI 3 **correctly** carries `[STAThread]` + `ComWrappersSupport.InitializeComWrappers()` + the `DispatcherQueueSynchronizationContext` setup — this matches the SDK's auto-generated `Main`. `[STAThread]` is **required**, not a bug. If you have a hand-written entry point and don't need single-instancing/redirection, the simplest path is to delete it and let the SDK generate `Main`.The Step 4 validator runs this same check (`Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` Section 7) and **fails** when the app registers but dies at startup, surfacing the captured signature in `.validator-diagnostics.txt`.
<shellId: 51 completed with exit code 0>

24.

26.         {
27.             this.InitializeComponent();
28.         }
29. 
30.         protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs args)
31.         {
32.             Application.Current.UnhandledException += Current_UnhandledException;
33. 
34.             var window = new AdvancedCasting.MainWindow();
35.             MainWindow = window;
36.             window.Activate();
37.         }
38. 
39.         private void Current_UnhandledException(object sender, Microsoft.UI.Xaml.UnhandledExceptionEventArgs e)
40.         {
41.             if (MainWindow?.Content is Microsoft.UI.Xaml.FrameworkElement fe &&
42.                 fe.FindName("RootFrame") is Frame rootFrame)
43.             {
44.                 rootFrame.Navigate(typeof(UnhandledExceptionPage));
45.                 if (rootFrame.Content is UnhandledExceptionPage p)
46.                     p.StatusMessage = e.Exception.Message + e.Exception.StackTrace;
